DA-ICT Merit Scholarship
UG-Merit Scholarship

From the academic year (3025-76), the
Institute has added a new scholarship category,
"institute Fellowshio” under which students at
the time of admissions will be awarded
multiple different fellowships aamely monk
based (JJEE AIK and ACPC}, only girl students
(EE AIR and ACPC rank) and out station school
hoard Loppers. The amount of the scholarship
will be equal ta 100% of the tuition fee for ihe
duration of the program. All students in this
category will continue to reecive the
scholarship subject to their satisfying the
academic performance criteria in all
SUbSequent semesters. In each catepory five
scholarships are being offered.

During the academic year 2024-25, a total of 34
students (those who scored a high Semester
Performance Index (SPI)) were awarded the
Merit Scholarships, The total amount disbursed
is Rs. 40.30 Lakhs.

~ UG-DAFS Merit Scholarship

The DAFS Merit Scholarships are awarded to
the top five students admitted to the B.Tech
program having 7 (seven) or above SPI in their
respective semesters,

During the academic year 2024-25, a total of 21
students were awarded the Merit Scholarships.
The total amount disbursed is Rs, 15 Lakhs/-.

PG-Merit Scholarship

The Merit Scholarships are awarded to the
students admitted to the PG programs like
M.Sc. {IT}, MSc. (05), M.Sc. (AA) and M_Des.
having the highest 5P! in their respective
Semesters.

During the academic year 2024-25, atotal of 25
students were awarded the Merit Scholarships.
The total amount disbursed is Rs, 27.24 Lakhs.

DA-NCT Merit-cum-Means Scholarship
UG Merit-cum-Means Scholarship

In Merit-cum-Means scholarship, program
wise eligible students will be awarded 70% and
50% fee reimbursements based on their
income criteria i.e. up to Rs. 6 lakh and up to
Rs. 8 lakh, respectively.

During the academic year 2074-25, a total of 36
students were awarded the Merit-cum-Means
Scholarships. The total amount disbursed is Rs.
50.70 Lakhs.

A university named as Dhirubhai Ambani Institute of Information * and
Communication Technology (Private University), Gandhinagar has been established by Act
No. 6 of 2003 of State Government of Gujarat and notified through the State Gazette vide
Notification No. VSP-2001-1-ITD dated 21-4-2003. The said university has been included
in the list of universities mantained by the University Grants Commission under Section- 2
(f)ofthe UGC Act, 1956.

However, the above university is not eligible to receive any assistance from
Univerenty Grants Commission and any other sources funded by the Government of India
under Section 12 (B) ofthe UGC Act, 1956.

MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT

Zisk Mia verde?
This AGREEMENT made and entered into on Day of ss 2022 between

DHIRUBHAl AMBANI INSTITUTE OF INFORMATION & COMMUNUCATION
TECHNOLOGY, GANDHINAGAR (here in after referred to as “DA-IIGT") as party of
FIRST PART and NODAL AGENCY

ANC

ANAND AGRUCULTURAL UNIVERSITY, Anand (hereinafter referred to as “AAU") as
a pany of SECOND PART

The party of First part and Second part jointly called and referred to as “The
PARTIES”

Dhirubhai Ambani Institute of Information & Communication Technology was
established under Gujarat Act No. 6 of 2003 and is engaged in research, teaching &

training in information and communication technology & allied disciplines.

Anand Agricultural University was established under Gujarat Act Ne. 5 of 2004 ts
angaged in education. research and extension activities related to Agriculture,
Agricultural Engineering, Horticulture. Agricultural & Food Product Processing, Home
Science, Agricultural information Technology and Agricultural Business and
Management.

Now, therafare in continuation of above MoU. the parlias hereto enter into another
agreement ta implement a PG program jointly, The program is for 2 years in M.Sc.
{Agriculture Analytics).

\asayeh 1

|
+



Objectives

1. Design, develop and deliver programme ensuring required quality which in turn
will laad to professional skills as per the industry requirements.

2. Working closely with industry and academia for establishing the curnculum as
per iis requirements in order to enhance the quality of education in the PG
prograrn.

3. Enhancing skills of students/aspirants through internship and vocational training.
biliti f rties

1. The PARTIES shall provide the available infrasiructure & basic anvenities such
as resitiential facility, food. internet. @c.; al a reasonable cost io be decided by
respective parties.

2. The PARTIES shall nominate coordinators as a point of contac! (POC) for proper
operation of the agreement from each of the two institutes.

3. The PARTIES shall utilize its strength to align with the programme and initiatives
to. meet the objectives of the programme, It shall accordingly design and deliver
various programmes within (he overall scope of the agreement,

4, The qualily delivery is the responsibility of The PARTIES and The PARTIES
would have to have in-house or engage the required faculty as per the
requirement, in conjunction with the Programme,

5. The PARTIES shall design, develop and provide required course matenals &
practical raining to the students.

6. The PARTIES shall market the programmes to make them successful, It shall
also guide and support the students for proper placement.



Course Duration and Conduct of course Se

Te

4.

This PG program having two years of duration with four samesters.

Semester One & Semester Four shall be conducted by the party of First part thal
ig DAT,

Semester Two shall be conducted by the Indian Institute of Remote Sensing
(RS), Dehradun. the another pariner of the party of First Part i.e. DAICT.

Semester Three shall be conducted by the party of second part thal is “AALS”

Financial arrangement

1

Z.

Course fee shall be decided in mutual agreement by all the PARTIES jointly.

For course under the purview, fees from the students for all four semesters shall
be collected by the party of First Part that is DA-IICT.

Tuition fees collected by the DA-IICT for semester three shall be reimbursed to
respective party which conduct that particular semester of ihe program,
immediately after the commencement of semester and invoice raised by the
respective party that is AAU.

All other feas like Registration fees, Value added course fees shall be collected
by the party of first part being a nodal agency and will remain with party of first
part only. Fees for the guest lectures, maximum 10 during the program, will be
bome by the first part that is DAICT, Hostel fee will be collected al respective
instiuties. Al other expenses during the semesler will be the bome by the
respective institules,

Promotional costs like seminars, workshops, symposia, conferences and
meetings shall be bome by the party of first part that is DA-IICT,

Duration and Termination of Agreement

This agreement shall be effective from the date of signing and shall remain in force
unless discontinued by any of the two parties,



The PARTIES has no right to terminate the agreement before ihe completion of all the
four semesters of a batch. At least three month prior notice is required for termination
before ihe start of the new batch of the program.

Force Majeure

The Parties shall not be liable for any failure to perform any of its obligation under this
MoA if the performance is prevented, hindered or delayed, by reason of war, or hostility,
acts of the public enemy, civic commotion, sabotage, act of stale or direction from
Statutory Authority, explosion, epidemic, quarantine restriction, strikes and lockouts.
fire, floods, natural calamities or any act of God (hereinafter referred to as event).
provided notice of happening of any such events given by the affected party to the
other, within 27 calendar days from the date of occurrence thereof, neither party shall,
by reason of such event, be entitles to terminate the MoU, nor shall either party have
any such claims for damage against the other, in respect of such non-performance or
délay in performance. Provided service under the MoU shall be resumed as soon as
practicable, aller such event comes to an end or ceases to exist. Each party shall
promptly infonm the other of ihe existence of Force Majeure event and shall consull

logether ta find a mutually acceptable solution.

Arbitration

In the event of a dispute between the parties of this agreement, arising oul of. or relating
to this Agreement, ils interpretation or performance hereunder, the parties shall exert
their best efforts to resolve the dispute amicably through negotiations.

in case of any disagreement and dispute and the parties fall to mutually resolve the
issue: parties to this agreement shall appoint an Arbitrator that is mutually agreeable
and shallbe settled as per Indian Arbitration Act,

Further, in case the parties failed to resolve the dispute; this Agreement shall be
governed by and construed in accordance with the law within the stale of Gujarat, India

Jurisdiction shall be Court of Ahmedabad.

leaching & fraining in information and communication technology & allied disciplines through
tS representative’ authorized signatory, Or. K. S. Dasgupta, Director having its headquarter
located at Gandhinagar (hereinafter referred to as “DA-IICT) which terms or expression shall
Unless @xcluced by OF repugnant to the subject or context, mean and include its successer-in-
office, adrmunistrators of permitted assignees) of the First Part:

AND

INDIAN INSTITUTE OF REMOTE SENSING - a constituent Unit of INDIAN SPACE
RESEARCH ORGANISATION (ISRO) engaged in training. education and research activities
In the field of Remote Sensing and Geo-information science. and their applications since 1966
Inrough its representative authorized signatory. Dr. R. P. Singh, Director having its ragistered
office located af Dehradun (hereinafter referred to as “IIR'S” which term or expression, unless
excluded by Gf repugnant to the subject or context. shall mean and include its successar-in-
office administrators or permitted assignees) of the Second Part.

The party of First part and Secend Part shall be individually called as “PARTY” and
collectwely referred to as “The PARTIES" in this Memorandum of Agreement (Moa),

WHEREAS the PARTIES of first part & second pari have entered into a Memorandum of
Understanding (MoU) on 29" day of November 2021 and by which they agreed to develop
academe and research cooperation in teaching, training and research for the advancement

and dissemination of learning with cerlain terms & conditions as contained in that MoU.

Now therefore in continuation of above MoU, the PARTIES hereto enter into Memorandum of
Agreement (MoA) to implement a Post Graduate (PG) program. This PG program shall be
referred as M.Sc. in Agriculture Analytics in this document. The M.Sc in Agnculture



Analytics is a two-year (four semesters) PG program and the degree will be offered by DA-
ICT, Gandhinagar,

Objectives

1 To disseminate knowledge and provide learning opportunities to aspiring students
through a PG program (M.Sc. in Agriculture Analytics)

2 Curricula building for M.Sc. in Agnculture Analytics in close coordination between the
PARTIES to enhance the quality of education.

a Enhancing skills of students through internship and vocational training, which shall be
arranged by DA-IICT

Roles and Responsibilities of the PARTIES

1 The PARTIES shall provide the available infrastructure such as library. classrooms and
laboratory facilities during the course of the program.

Z The PARTIES shall nominate coordinators from their respective institutes as a Point of

Contact (Pot) for proper execution of the agreement.

The PARTIES shall design, develop and provide required course material and practical

‘ta

training to the students.

ne

The PARTY of the First part shall be responsible for the approval of the M.Sc in

Agriculture Analytics program from the appropriate Authorities! institutions

5 The PARTY of the Second part shall be responsible for the design, development and
implementation of the Second Semester.

6. The entire legal liability raised by students and all external stakeholders shall be dealt
by the PARTY of the First Part.

t No PARTY shall be allowed to use logo and/or advertise and/or publish the

commitments under this MoA without the prior written consent of the other Party

Course Duration and Conduct of Course Semesters

1 The PG program (M.Sc. in Agriculture Analytics) is of two-year duration with four
semesters.

Z Semester - One & Semester Four shall be conducted by the PARTY of First part, ie
‘DA-NCT



3 Semesier - Two shall ba conducted by the PARTY of second part i.e., “IRS”.

| Semester - Three shall be conducted by the Anand Agricultural University “AAU
Anand, Gujarat

5 The transcript of the students will mention the names of all the three Instituies where
leaching was done in-all the semesters.

Financial Arrangements

1 Course fee and the number of seats shall be decided in mutual agreement by the
PARTIES. jointly
Z The fee for the course (M Se in Agriculture Analytics) fram the eligible students for all

four semesters shall be collected by lhe Party of First Part that is DA-IICT

3 «semester course fee as collected by the DA-IICT shall be remitted to IIRS at least
two weeks pnor fo commencement of the Semester to meet the program expenditure.

a The students attending the 2“ Semester at IIRS shall be covered with health and
accident insurance schemes as provided by DA-NCT. Accordingly. the DA-IICT shail
inform the insurer that the students are attending the 2" Semester at IRS.

5 any student leaves the program during 2“ Semester, then the tuition fee shall not be
refunded to the student by lIRS

6 All other fees like Registration and Value-added course fees shall be collected by the
Marly of first part being a nodal agency and will remain with Party of first part only

=~

The fees for the guest lectures, maximum 6 - 7 nos. during the program shall be borne
by the first party Le., DA-IICT. During 2“ semester at lIRS. the honorarium for a
minimum of two and maximum of three guest lecturers shall be permissible. Towards
this, the focal point from |IRS shall forward the honorarium form to the focal point of DA.
CT and the honorarium shall be provided to quest speaker by DA-ICT with information
to the focal point of IRS

5 The expenditure towards the course material and field visits (if any) during 2" Semester
atliRS shall be borne by the Second Party ie., IRS.

The students shall be allowed to use the Mess/ Canteen facility of IRS as per prevailing
rales and rules applicable for regular students of IRS

ss

es

41


10. Promotional costs like seminars. workshops. symposia. conferences and meetings shall
be borne by the party of first part that is DA-IICT
11 RS would try to provide hostel facility to the girl students of the program

ij nd Termination of Agreement

This agreement shall be concurrently effective as per the Memorandum of Understanding
(MoU) signed by the PARTIES on November 29, 2021

The PARTIES has no right to terminate the agreement before the completion of ail the four
semesters of an ongoing batch. However, the fresh intake of students to the joint program shall
be governed by the praesent MoA and the MoU previously signed on November 29. 2021. Al
least (hree-month prior notice is required for termination before the start of new batch of the
program.

Limi f Liabilities

To the fullest extent permitted by law:

1 Neither Party is liable for loss of profits, business interruption. loss of business
information: economic loss, notional loss, or any olher indirect. incidental consequential
of special loss or damage. even if the loss or damage was caused, or contribuled to. by
that Party's negligence or breach of this: MoA; and

é Each Party's total liabilily to the other party for any other damage under this Mov ic
limited to the amount actually paid by that Party to the other party and for that particular

academic session.

Force Majeure
The PARTIES shall not be tiable for any failure to perform any of its obligation under this MoA

the performance is prevented, hindered or delayed, by reason of war. or hostilily acts of the
pUbIC enemy, civic commotion. sabolage, act of state or direction from Statutory Authority,
Explosion. Epidemic, quarantine restriction, strikes and lockouts, fire. floods. natural calamities
or any act of God (hereinafter referred to as event), provided notice of happening of any such
events given by the affected party to the other, within 21 calendar days from the date of
occurrence thereof, neither party shall, by reason of such event, be entitled to terminate the
MoA. nor shall either party have any such claims for damage against the other in respect of

Arbitration

nthe event of a dispule between the PARTIES of this agreement, arising out of. or relating to
this Agreement, its interpretation or performance here under, the PARTIES shail exert their
bes! efforts to resolve the dispute amicably through negotiations. In case of any disagreement
and dispute and the PARTIES fail to mutually resolve the issue within thirty (30) days, the
Matter fas to be referred to an Arbitrator identified by beth the parties. and the provisions of
Arbitration & Conciliation Act. 1996, as amended from time to time shall be taken in te
consideration for the appointment of Arbitrator. The Arbitrator to be named shall not be below
the rank of the Secretary to the Govt. of india. The language of the arbitration shall be English
and the decision of {he arbitrators shall be final and binding on the PARTIES. The venue of
Aroitration shall be Dehradun. The resulting award shall be in lieu af any other remedy Both
Parties rrevocably submit to the exclusive jurisdiction of the Courts in Dehradun. for any action
or proceeding regarding this MoA

Further. in case the PARTIES failed to resalve the dispute: this Agreement shall be governed
by and construed in accordance with the Indian laws.

Date: - 6" May 2024

To
Professor(Retd) Kiran Parmar,
Email: krparmar2006@yahoo,co,in

Dear Professor Parmar,

We are pleased to inform you that the competent authorities of the University
have decided to appoint you as an Ombudsperson of the University, as per the
provisions made under the UGC (Redressal of Grievances of Students)
Requiations 2023 published by the UGC Notification dated 11 April 2023,

As per Section 6(v) of the above referred Notification, your term of appointment
shall be for a period of three years from the date of your acceptance, or you attain

the age of 70 years, whichever is earlier.

All other terms of your appointment shall be as per the terms and conditions
mentioned in the above referred Regulations (copy attached).

Kindly send your acceptance by return email and oblige,
With high regards,

Sincerelyiydaurs,

Siddharth Svaminarayan
Executive Registrar

DA-IKCT Road, Gandhinagar, Gujaral, India 382007 | +91 79 6826 1700 | info@daiictac.in | wew gaiictac.in


Brief introduction of Ombudsperson

Prof, Kiran Parmar obtained his B.E (Electronics), M.E (Electrical) and Ph.D (Electrical) degrees
from The Maharaja Sayajirao University of Baroda . He possesses over 30 years of teaching
experience in various Engineering Colleges of Gujarat State like L. D. College of Engineering
(LDCE), Abmedabad, Government Engineering College(GEC) Gandhinagar, GEC Modasa and
Adani Institute of infrastructure Engineering, Ahmedabad. He has served as doctoral progress
review committee member for 12 Ph.D students of Gujarat Technological University. He is
Chairman, Board of Studies of Electronics and Communication Engineering at Gujarat
Technological University and Member, Board of Studies at DDU, Nadiad. He has authored
many Lechnical books including Basic Electronics, Signals and Systems , Audio-Video Systems
, Digital Electronics and Design of Satellite Communication Toolbox for MATLAB. He is also the
author of 3 books in the field of Engineering Education, namely, Engineering Teacher's
Handbook, Great Universities and Comparative Study of Online Courses.

During his academic career, Prof.Parmar had attended long term training programmes at UT
Delhi and (Sc, Bangalore. He had held positions of Head of the Department at LOCE
Ahmedabad, GEC Gandhinagar “GEC Modasa and Adani Institute of Infrastructure
Engineering, Ahmedabad. He was also holding additional charpe of Joint Director, Technical
Education, Gujarat State and had co-ordinated AICTE sponsored MODROB/World Bank
sponsored TEGIP projects al LOCE, Ahmedabad, GEC Modasa and GEC Gandhinagar .

His teaching interest areas are active learning and design of experiments for laboratory
teaching. His research interest areas are 6G Mobile Communications and Satellite
Communications.

DHIRUBHAI AMBANI UNIVERSITY, GANDHINAGAR

MINUTES OF THE TWENTY-THIRD MEETING OF THE FINANCE COMMITTEE

The 23™ meeting of the Finance Committee was held on 30" January 2025 in a hybrid
mode in the Board Room of the Institute.

The following were present

|) Professor Tathagata Bandyopadhyay, Director General, in Chair
2) Shn Shrikant Kulkami, Member

4) 6 Shin Umesh Dalal, Member

1) Professor Maniklal Das

Shri Shrikant Kulkarni and Shri Umesh Dalal, Members, participated in the meeting,
through the video conference mode.

The following items were discussed

ltem Ne. 4

To propose revisions to our existing fee structure for all the acacemic
programmes and the hostel fees for the students to be admitted in academic
year 2025-26

Background Note:

The Education Department, Government of Gujarat, vide its notification dated 4th
January 2022 has declared seven private universities in the State of Gujarat as Centre
of Excellence (COE) for three years. We are one of the seven
universities. Subsequently, by a notification dated 7th March 2023, the COEs are
exempted from certain provisions of the Gujarat Professional Technical Educational
Colleges or Institutions (Regulation of Admissions and Fixation of Fees) Act 2007. As
one of the conditions of the said notification, COEs are required to notify/deciare the
fee structure for the professional programs (BE-BTech, ME-MTech for us) to the
Appellate Committee, the proposed fee structure for the next academic year. As per
one of the provisions, if the AC within three weeks of receipt of the declaration finds
that the proposed fee structure substantially constitutes profiteering, the declaration
shall be referred back with comments to enable such institution to modify the fee
structure accordingly and shall not implement the proposed fee structure.

Accordingly, for the last AY 2024-25, after the due internal process, declared and
informed the Appellate Committee (AC) about our revised fees for the BTech and
MTech Programmes vide our letter dated Sth February 2024 (copy attache

vide its letter 14th August 2024 approved our proposed fee stpset



programs (copy attached). A statement showing the fee structure (increase in
comparison to the previous year) of our all academic programs and hostel fees for the
AY 2024-25 is also attached for reference.

A comparative statement of fees for the AY 2024-25 by the five COEs for their BTech
Program, including DAIICT, is attached herewith for your kind perusal. In the said
statement, from the current websites of Ahmedabad University and PDEU, we have
also mentioned the proposed fee for their BTech Program, published for the next A'Y
2025-26 admissions.

Matter for consideration:

The Finance Commitee, as per the directives given by the Board of Governors in its
18th January 2025 meeting, is requested to consider and propose revisions ta our
existing fee structure for all the programs for the students to be admitted in AY 2025-
26, and also for the existing batches of the students,

Resolution:

The Committee was appraised with the relevant Notification of Education Department,
Government of Gujarat and prescribed process described in it for the Centre of
Excellence institutions for the professional programmes offered by the Institutes. The
Committee was also appraised with the following documents.

a) Our last academic year's fees structure proposed to the Appellate Committee
vide our letter dated 9" February 2024.

b) Supporting documents to be attached with our proposal, for example, CA
certified statements (of the previous year) of income from the tuition fee and
expenditures of permissible expenses, as per the SOPs prescribed by the State
Government, along with all related schedules and annexures towards imparting
education in the BTech and MTech Programs and a consolidated statement for
all other Programs, to work out per student actual expenditures incurred and
per student income received by the institute.

c) Appellate Committee's Order dated 14" August 2024 approving our proposal
to increase the tuition fees in the professional programmes (BTech and MTech).

d) Comparative statement of tuition fees charged by prominent COEs during the
last AY and the fees proposed by some of them for the next AY 2025-26.

After due deliberation and examining the above documents, the Commiltee
recommended the following fees for all our programs, per semester, for the students
to be admitted in academic year 2025-26.

For the MTech Program proposed tuition fee, it was recommended to increase the
teaching assistantship (stipend) from the existing Rs. 12.500/ to Rs. 15,000/ per
month. By this, the proposed annual tuition fees paid by the MTech students. i.e
Rs.1,60,000/ will be fully repaid by the institute to the sibs sludents by way of
teaching assistantship stipend amounts to them,



Name of the | Per Semester | Proposed Revisions per Semester
Programme tuition fees for | for AY 2025-26 (Rs.)

M. Sc. (AA)

As the meeting was called for the above matter only, there were no further agenda
item's, and the meeting ended with a vote of thanks to the Chair.

Policy Title
Academic Integrity Policy
Background

Dhirubhal Ambani Instivute af Infernation and Communication Technology — DANCT was estableshed
in 2001 ond was conferred university status in 2003. The OAICT is incheded in the list of Universities
maintained by the University Grants Conmmason.

DAUCT 4a nbember of the Association of indian Universities (AIL).

The National Assexsment and Jccreditation Council, Government of lndia has accredited DA-NCT with
an 4’ Grade in 2017.

DANCT being a research-led University, the faculty, students, and research staff are active in research
in several academic disciplines. Asa part of their rexearch and putticathons, the faculty, students are
the research staff access and make use of the significant amount of infermation available in online
and offline mode. The eave of online access also poses a serious concern of unacknowledged use of
others’ intellectual work = plagiarism: which must be curbed,

DALNCT tinmly believes that Academic integrity and ethical research are pivotal in ensuring fair play In
nw knowledge creation and supports a policy as ameans to achleve compliance.

The poley ly framed bi pursuance of the Liniversity Grants Commission's (Promotion of Acumen
Integrity and Prevention of Plagiarism in Higher Educational Institutions) Regulations, 7018 published
nine Garette of india

This policy is owned by the Dhirubhal Ambani Institute of Information and Communication Technoiogy
refered bo as “DANCT’ hereafter.

Responsible Office

the Dean (Academic Program) will hawe the overall responsibility for creating. implementing and
updating the policy.

Approved By
This policy has been approved by the Board of Governors of DAINCT.

Palley Objectives

1. To create awareness about the significance of academic integrity in the commuct of research
and submission of the thesis, discertation, and other research publications.

2 To prevent academic misconduct including plagiarism in academic writing among students,
faculty, and research staff by providing timely information, technical support, and guidance

3. To strengthen all existing institutional mechanisms to envure responsible conduct of research

4, To develop systems to check and deter plagiarism that is bn compliadoe with the UGC
regulate.

Levels of Plagiarism!"

Plagearism would be quantified into the following levels in ASCEMtiNg order of severity for the purpose
of its definition:

Lewel 0: Similarities upto 10% - Minor similarities, no penalty (Zere Jevell
bevel 1: Similarities above 10% to 40% (Low level)
Lewel 2; Similarities above 40% to 60% (Mid-levell
Level 3: Similarities above 60% (High level}

The parameters of similarity witl be decided by the Academic Integrity Committee (AK)

Redrass procedure upon plagiarism detection
Presubmisson phase

The superviser will examine the similarity report of the to-De-submitted publication and if the level of
plagiarism is above level i, the student will be counseled for taking appropriate measures including
rewriting and re-phrasing the contents which are shown to be similar to the existing documents, The
process will be continued ull the similarity repevt clears the to-be-submitted publication.

Post-submission phase

iF any allegation of plagiarism is brought against a student podt-submission by either examiners or
publishers ne any other indevidual or agency, the matter will be referred to the AIC by the supervisor

The AIC shall follow the principles of natural justice while deciding about the allegation of plagaariam
against the Authors. The AIC shall submit its recommendations past the hearing keeping in view of
exclusions mentioned under the similarities check,

The redress process and the penalty will be dealt with ona case-to-cate basis based on the merits a
the complaints. The ADC will look into the merits of the complaint and hear out both sides fo resolve
the 6ans dnd when Fequnrene,

Penalties

Penalties in the cases of plagiarism shall be imposed on students pursuing studies at the level of
Masters and Research programs and on faculty & research staff of the DANCT only alter academic
misconduct on the part of the individual has been established without doubt, when all avers of
hooral have ben exhausted and individual in question hag been provided wath enough opportunity
ra defend himself of herself in a fair or transparent manner before the AIC.

Penalties in case of plagiarism in the submission of thesis and dissertations
The Academic Integrity Committee (AIC) shail impose a penalty after due process and in accordance

with pubdelines in the UGC notification |

Annexure-D

Report on Special Features
of Innovation and R&D





Annexure-D
Special features of innovation / R&D

Innovation in Program Design

Since its inception in 2001, the university has been a pioneer in offering the E. Tech
program in Information and Communication Technology (ICT)—a distinctive and
interdisciplinary program integrating computer science, electronics and VLSI design,
communication engineering, and humanities & social sciences. Over the years, the
program has established a strong reputation. consistently attracting high-achieving
etudents who excel as professionals and scholars, a tradition that continues to this
day.

Building on this legacy, the university has introduced several other interdisciplinary
programs, including M.Sc. in Data Science (2020), M.Sc. in Agriculture Analytics [a
unique program offered jointly with twe partner institutes viz. AAU, Ananad and IKS,
Dehradun, 2072), B.Tech. in Mathematics and Computing (2020), and B.Tech. in
Electronics and VLSI Design (2023).

The curricula of all programs are regularly revised to remain aligned with emerging
industry trends and requirements, ensuring academic rigor and contemporary

relevanee.

Innovation in Teaching & Learning

Since its inception, the university has strongly emphasised students’ leaming through
research and hands-on projects. The tradition still continues. The university, during its
existence in the last 25 years, has been able to produce more than 125 alumni who
are cither founders or co-founders of successful companies.

A significant number of undergraduate research projects are being published in
reputed international journals. To further encourage scholarly pursuits, the university
has instituted a Research Excellence Award for students,

The emergence of new technologies, especially Al, demands that the mode of teaching
and learning in universities undergo a significant change. The questions arise: what
skills would be relevant in the coming years, and accordingly, how should we prepare
our students to face the formidable challenges in the future? With this fact in mind,
the university has recently set up a centre, SPARK (Supporting Pedagogy. Al,



Resources & Knowledge), to provide a university-wide platform for students and
teachers to come together and discuss the emerging issues and challenges in
teaching and learning. Some faculty members have already been using LLM as a co-
teacher in their courses and are making the students use different LLMs to learn
things that are peripheral to the course but not covered in the class in detail,
Report: SPARK

Faculty Research, Patents & Prototype Development

Faculty Research

* Ihe faculty members are research-intensive, reqularly publishing in high-quality
international journals and conferences. Many have secured competitive research
grants from agencies such as SERB, DoT, ISRO, DRDO, NBHM, ANRF, and GUICOST.
Recently, the university received CSR funding exceeding 213 crores to establish a
smart Energy Learning Centre (SELC), focused on energy efficiency and renewable
energy research. Around 20 faculty members are currently working on 17
interdisciplinary research projects applying Al, ML, Data Science, Electronics,
Communication, and Robotics to address challenges in the energy domain, Ihe total
external research funding received by the university stands al %23.9 crores,
List of publications - httpy/resourcecentre.dau.ac, in/infaculty. pub/year_ £025. html

« JEEE Student Branch, DAU Organizes the INDGenius Al Summit 2025
The IEEE Student Branch of DAU, in partnership with INDGenius Gujaral, hosted the
INDGenius Al Summit 2025 on September 14, 2025. The summit aimed to explore
the transformative role of Artifical Intelligence across diverse industries, enhance
awareness of recent advancements, and foster interaction between. stucients and
industry experts. It also sought to inspire interdisciplinary collaboration and guide
Students toward emerging carter opportunities in Al,

The event featured distinguished Chief Guests Mr, Ankush Sabharwal, Pounder
af BharatGPT and CoRoverai, and Shri Ajay Tomar (IPS, Retd.), Former
Commissioner of Police, Surat. The panel of eminent speakers included Prof. G.
Yenkatesh [Director, School of Technolagy, DAU), Mr Vivek Anand [lechD
Cybersecurity Lid.) MrRohit Kashyap (Maytree School of Entrepreneurship), and Mr,
Dhruvil Hapani (QX Global Group).

As part of the summit, a hackathon titled IntelliHack 2025 was organized on the
theme Al & Cybersecurity. Ten teams advanced to the final round and presented
their innovative solutions before a jury chaired by Mr. Vivek Anand, with prizes
awarded to the top teams,

With participation from over 700 students representing beading institutes of
Ahmedabad and Gandhinagar, the summit provided a vibrant platform for learning,
dileguc, and networking in Al The event underscored DAU's commitment to
nurturing a culture of innovation and interdisciplinary engagement in emerging
technologies.

Dhirubhai Ambani Centre for Entrepreneurship and Incubation (DCEI)
The university hosts the Bhirubhai Ambani Centre for Entrepreneurship and
Incubation (DCE!) — a Section 8 company dedicated to fostering interdisciplinary
collaboration and supporting students and faculty in translating innovative ideas into
impacttul ventures. The Centre actively conducts lectures, workshops, and interactive
sessions featuring entrepreneurs and innovators, often in partnership with student
clubs, to nurture a strong culture of innovation and entrepreneurship on campus.



i

Two noleworthy innovations emerging from DCE in recent times incluche:

Gait Tracker —.A Wearable Device for Analyzing Gait Patterns:

This project focuses on developing a sophisticated wearable system for long-term gait
analysis. It also includes a mobile application that allows users and researchers to
access, visualize, and interpret gait data, contributing ta the creation of a digital
repository for future research and medical studies.

Robot Beak — A Bird Rehydrating Prodigy:

Designed to tackle the growing issue of bird dehydration amid rising temperatures, this
project offers a sustainable, automated solution that uses atmospheric water
harvesting technology to provide water to birds. It overcomes the constraints of
traditional, manually refilled feeders, demonstrating a fusion of environmental
sensitivity and technological innovation.

SPARK — Supporting Pedagogy, Al, Resources & Knowledge
DAU Centre for Teaching and Learning

Ohirubha Ambani University announces creation of a Centre for Teaching and Learning, called
SPARE — Supperting Pedagogy, Al, Resources & Knowledge. SPARK will function as.a university-wide
resource for bringing together resources and research to make pedagogy more impactful and
inclusive. Listed below are a non-ehaustive representative set of acthities af the centre for the
stake holders:

Functions of SPARK:

Coliect and disseminate teaching- learning practioes from araund the world
conducl leaching learning workshops

Incerperation of Al and other tools in the teaching: learning process

data analytics and research on the teaching-learning processes

Share guidance on emerging needs and methods related to teaching and learning.

Lom Lal SL ee

Por students:

1. SPARK will help students sharpen their language comprehension skills through better use of
tools and methodology inside and outside the class

7, (Create a frcilitoted Study Groups, led by students who have previously excelled in the
course

For instructors:

1. Create community space where teachers interact, lear and support each other,
2. Offer workshops for instructors — fram designing accessible syllabi and effective pedagogy.

Same Potential Activities:

1. Expert teams to conduct Faculty Development Programs for DAU Faculty as well as teachers
of outside science and engineering collepe

2. Interactive sessions with experts from peer institutes on ideas/thoughts /personal
experenoes in educathon

3. Oftentation program for TAs on a periodic basis

4, Faciliiate a mentoring program for new faculty so that they are better equipped to integrate
in the Institute educational process. Offer assistance with their course design, syllabus
preparation, teaching strategies and effective communications.



Structure:

SPARK will be administered by a Coordinator / Chairand a Joint Coordinator / Vice Chair, suppartind
a group of student volunteers and a secretary. SPARE will invite and offer relevant projects to the
faculty members. Centre will provide logistic support to these projects and help propagate the
outcomes of the project to the stake holders.

Prof Sanjay Srivastava 6 appointed as the Coordinator and Prof V. Sunitha as the Joint Coordinator of
Lhe centre.

Director General, DAW

SPARK Organization and Resources

Administrative structure

Centre will have a Choir / Coordinater and a Vice Chair / Joint Coordinator. They will be responsible
for floating and managing the projects and administering the resources of the centre,

in addition, the Centre will have a secretary (part-time) and a small group of student volunteers to
manage its activities.

Operations

Centre will both solicit and float interesting and innovative project of relevance to DAU and
academic in general. It will support a number of projects and provide logistic support for cxculing
the project and dissemination and implementat ion of key results coming out of these projects.

Space
IT is suggested that the current Smart City Lab (CEP) be converted inte the SPARE centre,
Budget

An initial budget of Rs 5 lakhs to support (a) External visitors/talk, [b) student volunteers, and (c)
pnopect expenses consumables.



SPARK Plans for Autumn, 2025-26

SPARK is embarking on an exciting journey te explore the integration of
Al in teaching and learning. We're launching a pilot study to investigate

how AI can enhance the teaching experience, and we would love for you
to be a part of this effort.

Why are we so gung-ho about it?

This pilot study will place us at the forefront of innovation in education,
helping us understand the transformative potential of Al in the classroom.
As a participant and contributor, you'll have the opportunity ta:

* Explore cutting-edge Al tools tailored for teaching and learning

- Contribute to meaningful research that will shape the future of
education at DAU

* Experiment with new teaching methods that could enhance
student engagement and learning outcomes

What's Involved?

« Duration: Autumn 2025-26

« Expectations: Participants will integrate Al tools into their

courses, share feedback, and engage in regular reflection sessions
(Say, once every two weeks),

How to Get Involved:

If you're interested in being a part of this exciting new journey, please
join us at the CEP conference room at 4 pm on Friday, 8th August, for an
informational session. We are seeking a diverse group of faculty members
from various disciplines, and your participation would be invaluable.

If you have any questions or need more information, feel free to reach
out to one of us.

Thank you for considering this opportunity to be part of something
innovative and transformative!

PhD and MTech TAship Bootcamp 2025

New PhD and MTech Teaching assistants were invited for a TA boot-camp and
orientation session on July 24, 2026. Resource persons were:

Faculty:
Prof V. Sunitha
Prof Ankush Chander
Prof Sanjay Srivastava,

Senior TAs:
Bhavin Makwana ID 202221005
Anustha Singh ID 202221009
Gulafsha Bhatti ID 202021005

In the two-hour long interactive session, we explained the duties of the TAs,
their modes of interaction with the students, and the ethical issues involved in
the TA-student relationship. Through a series of question-answers, these
concepts were expanded and illustrated.

The session has been recorded and an edited version is being prepared asa
resource for the future TAship orientation session.

Email text for the faculty meeting with SPARK
June 23, 2025

Dear Colleagues,

A month ago, our Institute announced the creation of SPARK - DAU
centre for teaching and learning. While we plan to hold a faculty meeting
early next semester to get your inputs and discuss the roadmap of the
centre, this mail is to get your help in starting some important activities
right away.

The centre plans to create a mechanism to run orientation programs for
the (a) TAs and (a) new faculty members. These programs are expected
to run every semester and we wish to create documents, recordings etc
to support the live sessions from our faculty. We request faculty
members who wish to contribute to this program to reply to this mail. We
will hold a meeting sometime next week to flesh out the details.



24 July, 2025
Dear Students,

You are embarking on a journey into teaching through your
TAship, To keep this journey safe and comfortable, SPARK is
organising a bootcamp for you. SPARK (Supporting Pedagogy,
AI, Resources, and Knowledge) is DAU's centre for teaching-
learning. The bootcamp will

start at: 3.30 pm

end at: around 5.30 pm

on: Thursday, 24th July

be at: CEP 102 (aka Mini-auditorium)

We hope to see you there. Meanwhile, if you have any
questions, you may write to Prof. Sanjay Srivastava
(sanjay_srivastava) or me (v_suni).

Annexure-D
Special features of innovation / R&D

Innovation in Program Design

Since its inception in 2001, the university has been a pioneer in offering the B. Tech
pragram in Information and Communication Technology (ICT)—a distinctive ariel
interdisciplinary program integrating computer science, electronics and VLSI design,
communication engineering, and humanities & social sciences. Over the years, the
program has established a strong reputation, consistently attracting high-achieving
students who excel as professionals and scholars, a tradition that continues to this
day.

Building on this legacy, the university has introduced several other interdisciplinary
programs, including M.Sc. in Data Science (2020), M.Sc. in Agriculture Analytics [a
unique program offered jointly with two partner institutes viz. AAU, Ananad and lS,
Qehradun, 2072), B.Tech. in Mathematics and Computing (2020). and B.Tech, in
Electronics and VLSI Design (2023).

The curricula of all programs arc regularly revised to remain aligned with emerging
industry trends and requirements, ensunng academic rigor anc contemporary

relevance,

Innovation in Teaching & Learning

Since its inception, the university has strongly emphasised sbhudents’ leaming through
research and hands-on projects, The tradition still continues. The university, during its
existence in the last 25 years, has been able to produce more than 125 alumni who
are either founders or co-founders of successful companies,

A significant number of undergraduate research projects are being published in
reputed international journals. To further encourage scholarly pursuits, the university
has instituted a Research Excellence Award for students.

The emergence of new technologies, especially Al, demands that the mode af teaching
and learning in universities undergo a significant change. The questions arise: what
skills would be relevant in the coming years, and accordingly, how should we prepare
our students to face the formidable challenges in the future? VVith this fact in mind,



the university has recently set up a centre, SPARK (Supporting Pedagogy, Al,
Resources & Knowledge), to provide a university-wide platform for students and
teachers to come together and discuss the emerging issues and challenges in
teaching and learning. Some faculty members have already been using LLM as a co-
teacher in their courses and are making the students use different LLMs to learn
things that are peripheral to the course but not covered in the class in detail.

Report: SPARK
Faculty Research, Patents & Prototype Development

Faculty Research

@ he faculty members are rescarch-intensive, regularly publishing in high-quality
international journals and conferences. Many have secured competitive research
grants from agencies such as SERB, DoT, ISRO, ORDO, NBHM, ANRF, and GUJCOST.
Recently, the university received CSR funding exceeding 213 crores to establish a
Smart Energy Learning Centre (SELC), focused on energy efficiency and renewable
energy research. Around 20 faculty members are currently working on 17
interdisciplinary research projects applying Al, ML, Data Science, Electronics,
Communication, and Robotics to address challenges in the energy domain. The total
external research funding received by the university stands at 223.9 crores.

On-Campus Entrepreneurship and Innovation Activities

Student-Led Initiatives

A vibrant cullure of innovation thrives on campus through active student bodies such as the
Business Club, Al Club, and IIE Student Chapter. These groups organize an array of events
year-round—including workshops, hackathons, and compelitions—centered around
entrepreneurship, emerging technologies, and Al-driven innovation, ensuring the campus
remains dynamic and intellectually engaging.

@ IEEE Student Branch, DAU Organizes the INDGenius Al Summit 20275
The IEEE Student Branch of DAU, in partnership with INDGenius Gujarat, hosted the
INDGenius Al Summit 2025 on September 14, 2025. The summit aimed to explore the
transformative role of Artificial Intelliqence across diverse industries, enhance
awareness of recent advancements, and foster interaction between students and
Industry experts. It alse sought to inspire interdisciplinary collaboration and guide
students toward emerging career opportunities in Al,

The event featured distinguished Chief Guests — Mr. Ankush Sabharwal, Founder of
BharatGP Tl and CoRover.ai, and Shri Ajay Tomar (IPS, Retd.), Former Commissioner of
Poheo. Surat. The pana of eminent speakers inclided Prof..G. Venkatesh (Director,
school of Technology, DAU), Mr. Vivek Anand (Tech Cybersecurity Lted.), Mr.Rohit
Kashyap (Maytree School of Entrepreneurship), and Mr. Dhruvil Hapani (Q% Global
Group).

As parlof the summit, a hackathon titled IntelliHack 2025 was organized on the theme
Al & Cybersecurity. Ten teams advanced to the final round and presented their
innovative solutions before a jury chaired by Mr. Vivek Anand, with prizes awarded
to the top teams.

With participation from over 700 students representing leading institutes of
Ahmedabad and Gandhinagar, the summit provided a vibrant platform far learning,
dialoguc, and networking in Al The event underscored DAU's commitment to
nurturing a culture of innevation and interdisciplinary engagement in emerging
technologies.



Dhirubhai Ambani Centre for Entrepreneurship and Incubation (DCEI)

The university hosts the Dhirubhai Ambani Centre for Entrepreneurship and
Incubation (DCEI) a Section & company dedicated to fostering interdisciplinary
collaboration and supporting students and faculty in translating innovative ideas into
impactful ventures. The Centre actively conducts lectures, workshops, and interactive
sessions featuring entrepreneurs and innovators, often in partnership with student
clubs, to nurture a strong culture of innovation and entrepreneurship on Campus.

Two noteworthy innovations emerging from DCEI in recent times include:

@ Gait Tracker — A Wearable Device for Analyzing Gait Patterns:
This project focuses on developing a sophisticated wearable system for long-term gait
analysis, It also includes a mobile application that allows users and researchers to
aceess, visualize, and interpret gait data, contributing to the creation of a digital
repositery for future research and medical studies.

® Robot Beak — A Bird Rehydrating Prodigy:
Designed to tackle the growing issue of bird dehydration amid rising temperatures, this
project offers a sustainable, automated solution that uses atmospheric water
harvesting technology to provide water to birds. It overcomes the constraints of
traditional, manually refilled feeders, demonstrating a fusion of environmental
sonsitivity and technological innovation.

Website link: http://ceid.dau.ac.in/

41. Abstract of the invention

Prof. Biswajit Mishra
Internet of Animals in Resource Constrained Regions

The dairy industry is an important part of Indian economy providing dairy products worldwece.
According to the latest research carried by IMARC group, the market size of dairy products i India
was approximately INR 10,527 billion in 2019 and among various dairy products, milk was considered
to be the major source of income. Despite being largest milk producer, India has managed to capiure
only 1% of the global dairy trade because; high milk production in India is due to larger cattle population
raiher than good milk yield. In order to increase economic benefits and satisfy the growing demands,
it becomes critical factor for the dairy cullivators to stimulate milk production of dairy cows

in dairy industry. poor detection of estrus limits high reproductive efficiency in the calle. The risk
factors include milk yield, lameness, poor nutrition, and herd size. With the cattle showing fewer signs
of estrus and for a shorter duration in the 21 day cycle, it becomes difficult for farmers fo identify estrus
by visual observations especially in case of larger herd size. Currently, the devices used for caltle
monitoring are imported, limited in services and are loo expensive. Moreover, due to its continugus
moritoring, these devices offen suffer from poor battery lie and are a deterrent for adoption of these
devioas. In this context, we develop an indigenous solution incorporating ultra-low power hardware
with energy autonomy so that it can be termed as a battery-less device (because of its Jong lashing
and no requirement for charging of ihe battery during the life time of the device) and soltware design
thal has the competitive advantage of being a thorough estrus monitoring and Getection aid at a low
cost and has potential to be extended for other applications within the dairy industry

ye aim to give a voice to the dairy farmers who offen miss on reaching out to veterinarians during the
golden hours for insemination due to the over reliance on manual tracking. By facilitating dairy farmers
with the real-time estrus detection system, dairy farmers will be able to manage langer herds remotely
with increased accuracy. As short estrus or estrus during evening or night could be possibly missed
by visual observations, this system will provide continuous surveillance of dairy cattle 24 h/day to
automatically and accurately identify cattle in estrus state. This will reduce the number of artificial
inseminations and use of hormones needed to impregnate the cow, preventing extended calving
interval thereby increasing the profitability of the farmers.

We have developed a battery powered step detection system which includes a low-power IMA cariit for
step detection, a transceiver for communication and microcontroller as @ controfing head. (see Fig 1)

2. Abstract of the invention

Prof. Tapas Kumar Maiti, Prof. Sujay Kadam and Prof. Rahul Mishra

ioT based Automation and Energy Monitoring for Coal Mines

The coal mine is a large and dynamic geographical structure. It requires mining machinery and
equipment to be connected to the mine's WiFi network for effeclive operations, For this purpose,
‘mobile! WiFi tewers/roulters are deployed through the mine as per the requirement in a given zone in
ihe coal mine. The coal mine Wi-Fi towers rely on a solar-battery combination that often faces charge
depletion, particularly during the night. Currently, battery levels must be checked manually, leading to
operational inefficiencies and potential connectivity disruptions. Our loT monitoring system aims to
solve this by automating real-time battery monitoring and reporting, ensuring prompt battery
replacement and minimizing service interruptions, using laT devices and sensors. This also makes
the mine operation more sustainable.

Our team comprised of Prof. Tapas Kumar Maiti, Prof. Supay Kadam and Prof. Rahul Mishra (now at
(iT Paina) have successfully deployed four loT prototype devices on-site (installed on Wi-Fi network
trolleys TNO8, TN20, TN28, and TN33) to monitor battery levels in realtime. This is to determane the
expected performance of the devices on field and to incorporate the suggestions and feedback arising
from the extensive testing of the devices,

The deployed devices enable real-time battery monitoring, accessible both on-site and from the contral
room through a dashboard, using the mine's local network

3. Abstract of the invention

Prof, P S Kalyan Sasidhar and Prof. Sujay Kadam

Awearnable device for Preventive Orthocare

The wearable devioe segment in the Indian healthcare sector is witnessing a rapid growth, The market
has seen as 53.3% growth YoY [1]. Smartwatches. smar rings, and smart bands from multiple
manufacturers are targeting the consumer market and consumers are embracing them. Despite the
diverse models, the focus is on providing features such as indoor / outdoor walking, and different
exercises modes. Data analylics include step counts, dislance covered calories burnt, heart rate, and
so on. However, none of the wearable devices in this segment cater to gail analysis,

Human gait follows a cyclical pattern due to the movement of both legs, Factors lke body weight, load
carried, or limb length discrepancy (LLD), cognitive degenerative diseases can all lead to
abnormalities in gait. Such factors lead to abnormal gaits like limping, dragging and scraping Lhe toes,
irregular leg movements and so on [2]. Such abnormal gait patterns could could lead fo dysfunctions
to the lower extremities, repetitive strain injury to the lower back, ostecarhritis, and so on. Currently,
the wearable market lacks a low cost abnormal gait diagnosing device. Allernalive technologies
include pressure sensor mats or plates [3] but their high cost limits their usage and availability to high-
end specialty hospitals. So the common solutions include medications or knee caps.

The proposed product is a non-invasive wearable device for preventive orthocare, The device is
portable, low-cost and would be designed as an article of normal wear, conveying a lHestyle statement.
while simullaneously containing technology embedded inside il that would enable its functional
performance including detecting sudden changes in gail and providing early diagnostics ol gait
abnornakles The stakeholders would be physiotherapists and orlhopaedics who can use this device
paired wih a mobile app al their clinics lo make accurale prognoses abou their pabents gant

4. Abstract of the invention

Prof. Rutu Parekh

ENVIRONMENTS

Qur research addresses a crilical gap in communicalion technologies for hilly terrains, where
traditional cellular networks are often unreliable or unavailable. By leveraging Long Range (LoRa)
technology, we have developed a robust communication system capable of transmitting text, images,
and sensor data over long distances, Our system employs multi-hop communication with strategically
placed repeaters to extend the communication range, achieving a maximum distance of 10.5 km under
experimental non line of sight flat fand conditions, It is expected to gel double the range in hilly line of
sight conditions.

A GUI facilitates transmission of text, image, sensor data and jocation of soldiers to the control room
A text froma sender is received by multiple recipients in addition to the contral roam. A map showing
location of each soldier is implemented in absence of cellular network. A robust algorithm is designed
to ensures the lossless transmission of images in a multi-hop environment. The findings of our
research have significant implications for military operations, enabling rebable and efficient
communication in remote and rugged terrains. This advancement not only enhances situational
awareness and operational coordination bul also ensures the safety and effectiveness of miliary
personnel in the field.

§. Abstract of the invention
Prof. Biswajit Mishra

Miniaturization and calibration of and (OT enabled ultra-low power consuming hear manitoring device
for long term monitoring of patient with cardiovascular diseases for resource constrained regions

Socio-Economic Importance

Cardiovascular diseases (CVDs) are the leading cause of death globally, with a disproportionale
burden on low- and middle-income countries. In these regions, healthcare systems face severe
constraints including inadequate infrastructure, shoriage of skilled personnel, and financial barriers to
care. Early detection through tools like electrocardiograms (ECGs) is crilical, yet remains inaccessible
to many due to cost, gender-related disparities. and geographical isolation, The development and
deployment of such devices could significantly transform health oulcomes and soqio-cconomec
development in ihese regions.

Healthcare Accessibility and Equity

+ Affordability: Traditional ECG machines are costly and require skilled technicians. A low-cost
alternative democralizes access, enabling widespread screening and early diagnosis.
especially in rural and underserved areas.

» Gender Independence: Many women in conservative or patriarchal communities avord ECG
tests due to cultural taboos around undressing for chest lead placement. 4 gender-
independent device {e.g., wearable or limbtead-based) reduces gender-based barners and
promotes equity in cardiac care

=» Decentralized Care: Such devices can be used in community health centers, mobile clinics,
and even at home, reducing cependence on tertiary hospitals and bridging urban-rural
disparities.

Public Health impact
» Early Diagnesis & Intervention. Timely identification of cardiac issues enables car
intervention, reqducing mortality, morbidily, and long-term drsability
- Epidemiological Surveillance and Reduction in Non-Communicable Disease
Deployment at scale helps build community4evel cardiac health data, informing tarpeted public
health interventions and resource allocation.

Economic Productivity

» Reduced Healthcare Costs: Preventive care reduces hospital admissions, surgical
interventions, and prolonged medication use, easing financial pressure on both individuals and
public health systems.

- Improved Workforce Health: Healthy individuals are more economically productive. Early
CVD detection can prevent years of lost productivity, particularly among working-age
populations.

+ Empowerment through Self-Monitoring: Empowering individuals with tools for sel-caro like
the proposed device, a smartphone-connected ECG fosters a culture of proactive healin
management, reducing dependency on overloaded public facilities.

Gender Empowerment
» Health Autonomy for Women: A gender-independent ECG allows women lo access cardiac
diagnostics without social or cultura! discomfort, encouraging better heallh-seeking behaviour
* Reduction in Gender Disparities in Caro: In many parts of Inca and Afmma, women are less
likely to be diagnosed or treated for hear disease. This dewice helps clase ihe diagnostic gap
particularly in maternal health where cardiac issues often go unnoticed.



innovation and Local Economic Development

* Stimulating Local Innovation Ecosystems: Developing and manufacturing such devices
locally can stimulate regional innovation, job creation, and technological self-reliance.

* Scalability for Global Health Markets: A successful low-cost ECG model can be scaled to
similar regions worldvede, positioning local innovators as global leaders in frugal health
technologies.

* Public-Private Partnerships: Thes initiative can spur collaborations between governrmnants,
NGOs, academic instilutions, and start-ups, enhancing health infrastructure sustainably

The proposed low-cos! ECG device offers transformative socio-economic benefits for resource.
constrained regions such as India. By bridging the gap in early cardiovascular disease detection.
reducing gender dispanties, and lowering healthcare costs, it becomes more than a medical tool—it
becomes an engine for social equity, public health progress, and economic resilience. For these
regions grappling valh a growing burden of non-communicable diseases, such innovations are ret just
tesirable—they are essential

Description of Salary Heads

a}

be

e)

di}

Fixed Yearly Cash Component: This is a total of the annual basic salary and add tiorsial
guaranteed cash components. These additional components include cash payments anc
allowances that are part of the annual package. The term gquarantead signifes that the amount
is certain unless there is an overall pay revision. The components falling under this salary head
are final and are not related to performance

One-time Cash Payment : This head indicates the value of the remuneration given to a
candidate as one-time cash benefit mostly at the time of joining.

Total guaranteed Cash Component: This is the sum of fixed yearly cash component and
one-thie cash payment,

Maximum Earning Potential: Thrs is the sum of total guaranteed.cash component, Mmeasiieit
parformance-linked variable and all other components of salary that are part of the offer. Ths
can include ESOPs, relocation allowances, long term compensation such a5 PF and other perks
as well,

Annexure-L
Dhirubhai Ambani University (DAU)

Zero Tolerance towards Ragging

Report on Anti-R ing Day & i-R ing Wee

Dhirubhai Ambani University, Gandhinagar, Gujarat
Date: 127th — 18th August 2025

1. Introduction

In accordance with the quidelines of the University Grants Commission (UGC) and as
part of the ongoing commitment to maintain a safe, inclusrve, and ragqging-free
environment, Dhirubhai Ambani University, Gandhinagar, observed Anti-Ragging Day
on 12th August 2025 and Anti-Ragging Week from 12th to 18th August 2025. Ihe
ewents were organized under the supervision of the Dean (Students) and with active
participation from the Student Body Government (SBG). faculty members,
administrative stalf, and students.

2. Anti-Ragging Day (12th August 2025)
a) Orientation for Undergraduate Students

The day began with a dedicated Orientation Session for newly admitted undergraduate
students, The session was addressed by the Dean (Students), who emphasized the
university's strict zero-tolerance policy towards ragging and highlighted the importance
of maintaining mutual respect, harmony, and inclusivity among students.

b) Screening of Informative Videos

Following the address, informative videos on the theme of anti-ragging were screened.
These videos sensitized students about:

@ The legal provisions and punishments related to ragging.

® [he rights and responsibilities of students.



® Support mechanisms and grievance redressal systems are available in the
University.

c) Awareness through Selfie-Stand Activity

To further promote awareness in an interactive and engaging manner, a themed sclfic

stand was installed. Students enthusiastically participated by clicking photographs and
sharing them, reinforcing the message of staying vigilant and committed te a ragging-
[ree campus,

d) Information on Committers & Vigilance Squads
Siudents were informed about the constitution of:
® Anti-Ragging Committees
@ Anti-Ragging Vigilance Squads

lhese bodies, constituted by the competent authorities, comprise faculty members,
administrative staff, and student representatives. Their role is to monitor student
interactions, handle complaints promptly, and ensure the safety and well-being of all,

¢) Role of the Student Body Government (SBG)

The SBG played an instrumental role by interacting personally with freshers. addressing
their queries, and assuring them of support. They reiterated that any student can
approach the cammiltlee or squad members without hesitation in case of any concern.

3. Anti-Ragging Week (12th - 18th August 2025)
Throughout the week, the university continued awareness activities, including:
® Informal interactions between seniors and juniors in supervised environments.

@ Display of posters and banners at key locations in the campus reiterating the
message “Say No ta Ragging”.

@ A street play was organised by students to spread awareness about anti-ragging
in an impactful and creative way. highlighting the importance of maintaining a safe
and inclusive environment,

® Circulation of UGC's online complaint portal and helpline details to all students,

4. Institutional Commitment



Dhirubhai Ambani University remains vigilant against any form of ragging. The
administration, faculty, and students collectively ensure a safe and friendly campus
environment, The university is determined to maintain its ragging-free status in the
future, thereby fostanng an atmosphere conducive to academic grawth and personal
development,

§. Conclusion

The observance of Anti-Ragging Day and Week was highly successful in reinforcing the
university's stance against ragging. The active involvement of all stakeholders
administration, faculty, SBG, and students—demonstrated a united front in promoting
respect, dignity, and camaraderie among peers.

At YourDOST, we use the Subjective Unit of
Wellness (SUV) as a qualitative metric to assess
the effectiveness of a counseling or emotional
support session, This metric captures the perceived
severity of the concern or issue a student is facing,
both before and after the session, directly from the
student's perspective,

How it Works:

Before the session begins, students are asked to
rate the severity of their concern ona scale from 1
to 10, where 1 represents minimal distress and 10
represents extremely high distress.

At the end of the session, the same scale is used to
measure their state post-intervention,

Red Flags at Dhirubhai Ambani University

The Red Flag Protocol is aimed to provide intensive help to clients who may be in crisis or may require a
multi-pronged approach. It is only in case of critical situations that we inform the organisation about a potential red
flag as it requires us to break confidentiality. The focus is on ensuring the client's well-being.

THEADHS

Sharadha (name changed) is a 24-year-old data engineer at an IT company. She recently completed her postgraduate studies
fram the Dhirubhai Ambani Institute of Information and Communication Technology (DA-IICT).

“At the beginning of the first semester, | met a guy who wanted to date but didn't want any commitment. | learned later that ne
was double timing me and another person, so | got out of it and moved on, but he started ruining my reputation with my
classmates, blaming me"Sharadha elucidates being anxious about the whole situation, and she also started having mental
breakdowns as she had nobody to give her emotional support. Every friend she made in college stopped talking to Sharadha
due to this issue. and she was also facing quarrels with her partner.



YourDOST Warrior Stories

"Lwas overwhelmed with
academics and grades and
couldn't perform well in
placements.

Therapy at YourDOST
brought me back to reality
and helped me focus on
the important things."

-PITANEHU.

Piyanshu is a 21-year-old student pursuing his Bachelor of Technology from DA-IICT. He is currently doing an internship in
Ahmedabad, Piyanshu also loves to write poetry and play tennis, which he pursues in his free time.

When Piyanshu left home for college, he faced some challenges. He found the curriculum to be not very practical but heavily
theoretical. Despite being interested in the subjects, he couldn't understand them well, which affected his grades. Eventually,
these issues also started affecting his placements in college.

Piyanshu was aware of his college's subscription to the YourDOST platform and went through the website. He found mast of
the self-help resources helpful. He decided to give counseling a try as well and started taking chat sessions with the experts
available, Ful) St :

Annexure-Q

Details about Rural

Annexure-Q

RURAL INTERNSHIPS

The Rural Internship is a mandatory requirement of the B.Tech curriculum. The objective is to
expose students to the social and economic realities of rural lives; sensitize them to the
vulnerabilities of rural communities, and facilitate an appreciation of the constraints and
opportunities presented by the development of rural India. A Rural internship is for three
weeks at the end of their Third Semester, during December vacation. A five-member Rural
internship Committee appointed by the Director is respansible for the Rural Internship.

For the academic year 2072-27023, two batches of students were enrolled for these
inkernishups.

fhe Blech 7070 and 7071 batches together comprised around 742 students, They were
placed in & States with 63 Non-Governmental Organizations, The Rural Internship period was
from December 14, 2022, to January 7, 2023. Details appear in Table 1.

Annexure-S

Students community service
group - Sambhav Activities





Annexure-5

Sambhav Activities

Organised a blood donation drive twite a year. We invite the Ahnedabad Civil Hospital
Blood Bank in the Autumn semester and the Indian Red Cross in the Winter semester,
Conducted a clothes collection/donation drive twice a year. The donated clothes,
blankets, shoes, etc., from the students, the staff, and the faculty are washed,
laundered, and distributed to the daily wage workers on and off campus

Regular visits to the orphanage in Gandhinagar. The DAU students spend time with the
orphan children participating in recreational activities, distributing gilts and sweets.
Conducted a book sale drive in which the used books are collected from the
graduating students and sold to the intaming students at a discounted rate, The
proceeds from this book sale drive are used to partially fund the orphanage visits

. Tithe of Project: Al-Based Detection and Defense Techniques for Deepfake Videos in
Forensic Applications

- Organization: Joint Proposal by Narnarayan Shastri Institute of Technology (AMiliated
with National Forensic Sciences University, MHA, Govt. of Inelia) and Dhirubhai Ambani
University, Gandhinagar.

» Mame: Prof. ( Dr.) Sailesh Survanarasan [yer (PL, SSET)

. Nature of Project (Check one}

a. Research, Development & Engineering (R.D-& EC) leading to producteon capabilits

b, Application oriented Research, Design and Development (R.DO&D) faving

ce. Basic RED

. Objective of the Project

The rapid evolution of artificial intelligence has revolutionized multimedia content creation.
Among its most striking developments is the emergence of deepfukes [/, 2), synthetic videos
generated using deep learning methods such as Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) [3]
and diffusion madels (4, 5, 6]. These models can manipulate of fabricate facial expressions
[7]. speech [8], and entire videos with remarkable realism, offen indistinguishable from
authentic content to the human eye. While such technology holds potential in entertainment,
education, and accessibility, its misuse has raised significant concerns in society.

Deepfakes have already been weaponized for political misinformation, identity theft, fraud,
harassment. and cybercrime. In the forensic domain, where video evidence is offen
considered a gold standard for truth, deeplakes pose a direct threat to justice and security.
Admissibility of digital video evidence in courts can be guestianed if reliable detection
methods are absent, undermining trust in judicial processes. The growing sophisticution of
pencrative models exacerbates this challenge: carly deepfakes contained visible anifaets and
temporal inconsistencies, bul modern architectures achieve smooth frame transitions, realistic
textures, and even physiological consistency (e.2.. eve blinking, lip syncing).

Traditional forensic methods and early detection approaches rely on visual anifacts or
handcrafted features, which quickly become obsolete as deeplake generation evolves. State:
of-the-art research indicates that robust solutions musi i multi- lewg leaming



Given this background, the proposed project secks lo design and implement a deep learning:
based framework for reliable deepfake detection and prevention tailored to forensic
applications. The novelty of this project fies in integrating spatial-spectral convolutional
analysis, Vision Transformers [14], multimodal fusion, and physiological sipaal cues into a
unified detection system, To ensure forensic validity, the framework will incorporate
explainable Al (MAI) modules (15, 16], generaling interpretable evidence that ean assist
investigators, law enforcement agencies, and courts. The project will also focus on curating
culturally and regionally relevant datasets to capture challenges specific to India, including
linguistic and ethnic diversity in video data,

In summary, this project addresses an urgent national and global need: the development ofa
robust, forensic-wrade deeplake detection and prevention system that not only achieves high
detection accuracy bul also provides trustworthy, explainable evidence for judicial and
security applications.

Develop robust deep learning models for deepfake video detection:
The first objective is to design and implement deep learning models that can detect
manipulated videos with high accuracy. Unlike traditional forensic methods, which often rely
on handcrafted features, our models will combine spatial analysis {image-level
inconsistencies), temporal analysis (motion coherence and frame dy namics), and frequency-
domain features (spectral irregularities) to capture both visible and imperceptible cucs of
manipulation, By integrating convolutional neural networks (CNNs), Vision Transformers
(ViTs), graph convolution networks (GCMs) and hybrid architectures, the models will aim to
achieve high sensitivity to manipulations while maintaining low false alarm rates, making
them reliable for forensic use.

Ensure cross-dataset generalization and resilience fo cmerging manipulation
techniques: Many existing detection sysiems perform well only on the datasets they were
trained on, bet fail when tested on unseen manipulations or datasets, To address this
limitation, this objective focuses on domain adaplation and transfer learming techniques,
allowing the models to generalize across dilferent sources, resolutions, and manipulation
styles, Additionally, continual fearing strategies will be explored to update models against
newly emerging deepfake generation techniques without retraining trom scratch, ensuring
long-term resilience.

Integrate explainability for forensic-grade evidence: For deeplake detection to be legals
admissible and trustworthy, it is essential to provide interpretable oulputs rather than binan
predictions. This objective will incorporate explainable AL(AAL) methods, sich as auencticn
maps, saliency visualization, and temporal inconsistency highlighting, to indicate which
regions or frames of a video show signs of manipulation. The outcome will be a system that
not only classifies a video as real or fake but also produces forensic-grade reports that can be
used as supportive evidence in legal investigations.

Develop preventive mechanisms to stop the spread of deepfakes: Beyond detection,
preventing the dissemination of manipulated media is equally critical. This objective will
explore digital walermarking techniques, content authentication systems, and blockchain-
based verification frameworks to track video authenticity across platforms, By embedding
invisible markers during video recording or implementing erypiagraphic authentication
pipelines, the system can prevent manipulated content from being misused, thereby
strengthening the reliability of digital media in legal and public contents.
